holds. From the Leibniz formula for the determinant, we have that

\det\bm{M}_{R,n}=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)Q_{R}(\pi).

(782)

From the partition
\mathcal{S}_{[n]}=\bigcup_{i\in[n]}T_{n}^{(i,1)}
(similar to the partition in (
741
)), then, it suffices to show that

\mathfrak{S}_{2,4,1}=\sum_{i\in[n]_{1}}\sum_{\pi\in T_{n}^{(i,1)}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)Q_{R}(\pi).

(783)

We proceed to show that (
783
) holds. We employ a similar technique to how we showed (
744
). Fix
i\in[n]_{1}.
By the change of variables
\sigma=\pi_{i}
(equivalently,
\pi=\sigma_{i},
since
(1\;i)^{-1}=(1\;i)
) we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[n]_{1}}\sum_{\pi\in T_{n}^{(i,j)}\cap T_{n}^{(1,1)}}\hskip-14.22636pt\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;i,1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in[n]_{1}}\sum_{\sigma\in T_{n}^{(i,1)}\cap T_{n}^{(1,j)}}\hskip-8.53581pt\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma_{i})\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\mathcal{R}_{\sigma_{i}(i)+1}Q_{R}(\sigma_{i};i,1)

(784)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j\in[n]_{1}}\sum_{\sigma\in T_{n}^{(i,1)}\cap T_{n}^{(1,j)}}\hskip-8.53581pt\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)\mathcal{R}_{i+\sigma(i)}\mathcal{R}_{\sigma(1)+1}Q_{R}(\sigma;i,1)

(785)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j\in[n]_{1}}\sum_{\sigma\in T_{n}^{(i,1)}\cap T_{n}^{(1,j)}}\hskip-8.53581pt\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)Q_{R}(\sigma)

(786)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\sigma\in T_{n}^{(i,1)}}\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)Q_{R}(\sigma).

(787)

Summing over all
i\in[n]_{1}
and noting the minus sign in the definition of
\mathfrak{S}_{2,4,1}
in (
773
), we obtain that (
783
) holds. Hence, equation (
775
) holds. We now show that the other parts give a vanishing contribution, i.e., that (
776
)–(
780
) all hold.

Equations (
776
) and (
777
) follow from the expressions we we give in (
759
),(
760
),(
767
),(
768
). For (
776
), we note that each
j
in the summand in (
759
) may be replaced with
\pi(1)
as
\pi\in T_{n}^{(1,j)}.
Then, as

\bigcup_{j\in[n]_{1}}T_{n}^{(1,j)}=\bigcup_{i\in[n]_{1}}T_{n}^{(i,1)}

(788)

are both partitions of the same set, namely, the set of permutations that do not fix
1,
equations (
759
) and (
768
) yield that
\mathfrak{S}_{1,2}=-\mathfrak{S}_{2,2},
i.e., (
776
) holds. For (
777
), the expressions in (
760
) and (
767
) show that
\mathfrak{S}_{1,3}=-\mathfrak{S}_{2,1},
i.e., that (
777
) holds.

Next, we show that (
778
) holds. Fix
i\in[n]_{1}
and
r\in[n]_{1,i}.
We will show that the following sum vanishes

\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\binom{r+\pi(r)}{2}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}\mathcal{R}_{r+\pi(r)-2}Q_{R}(\pi;i,r)=0.

(789)

As
\mathfrak{S}_{2,3},
according to equation (
769
), is a linear combination of such sums, we would obtain that
\mathfrak{S}_{2,3}=0,
i.e., that (
778
) holds. To show that (
789
) holds, we utilize that
\pi\mapsto\pi_{i}
is an automorphism of
\mathcal{S}_{[n]},
as follows. We have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\binom{r+\pi(r)}{2}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}\mathcal{R}_{r+\pi(r)-2}Q_{R}(\pi;i,r)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\binom{r+\pi(r)}{2}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}\mathcal{R}_{r+\pi(r)-2}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;1,i,r)

(790)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi_{i})\binom{r+\pi_{i}(r)}{2}\mathcal{R}_{\pi_{i}(i)+1}\mathcal{R}_{r+\pi_{i}(r)-2}\mathcal{R}_{\pi_{i}(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi_{i};1,i,r)

(791)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\binom{r+\pi(r)}{2}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(1)+1}\mathcal{R}_{r+\pi(r)-2}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;1,i,r)

(792)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\binom{r+\pi(r)}{2}\mathcal{R}_{r+\pi(r)-2}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;i,r),

(793)

so the vanishing in (
789
) holds. Hence, (
778
) holds.

Next, we show that (
779
) holds. We rewrite (
774
) as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{2,4,2}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{(i,j,k,\ell)\in[n]_{1}^{4}}\sum_{\pi\in T_{n}^{(i,j)}\cap T_{n}^{(1,k)}\cap T_{n}^{(\ell,1)}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\binom{k+1}{2}\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\mathcal{R}_{j+1}\mathcal{R}_{k-1}\mathcal{R}_{\ell+1}Q_{R}(\pi;1,i,\ell).

(794)

We fix
(j,k)\in[n]_{1}^{2}
and show the vanishing of each of the following sums

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,\ell)\in[n]_{1}^{2}}\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\mathcal{R}_{\ell+1}\sum_{\pi\in T_{n}^{(i,j)}\cap T_{n}^{(1,k)}\cap T_{n}^{(\ell,1)}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)Q_{R}(\pi;1,i,\ell)=0.

(795)

From (
794
), we may write
\mathfrak{S}_{2,4,2}
as a linear combination of such sums, so we would obtain that
\mathfrak{S}_{2,4,2}=0.
We show (
795
) next. We change variables as
\sigma=\pi\circ(i\;\;\ell)
in the inner sum in (
795
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in T_{n}^{(i,j)}\cap T_{n}^{(1,k)}\cap T_{n}^{(\ell,1)}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)Q_{R}(\pi;1,i,\ell)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in T_{n}^{(\ell,j)}\cap T_{n}^{(1,k)}\cap T_{n}^{(i,1)}}\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma\circ(i\;\;\ell))Q_{R}(\sigma\circ(i\;\;\ell);1,i,\ell)

(796)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\sigma\in T_{n}^{(\ell,j)}\cap T_{n}^{(1,k)}\cap T_{n}^{(i,1)}}\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)Q_{R}(\sigma;1,i,\ell).

(797)

Multiplying by
\mathcal{R}_{i+1}\mathcal{R}_{\ell+1}
then summing over
(i,\ell)\in[n]_{1}^{2},
we obtain that the quantity on the left hand side of (
795
) is equal to its negative. Hence (
795
) holds, and we obtain that
\mathfrak{S}_{2,4,2}=0.

Finally, we show that
\mathfrak{S}_{3}=0.
We may write

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{3}=

\displaystyle-\hskip-2.84526pt\sum_{i\in[n]_{1}}\hskip-2.84526pt\binom{i}{2}\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\hskip-2.84526pt\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\hskip-2.84526pt\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;i)-\hskip-2.84526pt\sum_{j\in[n]_{1}}\hskip-2.84526pt\binom{j}{2}\mathcal{R}_{j-1}\hskip-2.84526pt\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\hskip-2.84526pt\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(j)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;\pi^{-1}(j)).

(798)

We will show that for each
(i,j)\in[n]_{1}^{2},

\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;i)=0

(799)

and

\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(j)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;\pi^{-1}(j))=0.

(800)

Together, equations (
799
) and (
800
) imply in view of (
798
) that
\mathfrak{S}_{3}=0.
To show that (
799
) holds, we apply the automorphism
\pi\mapsto\pi_{i}
of
\mathcal{S}_{[n]},
from which we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;i)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;1,i)

(801)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi_{i})\mathcal{R}_{\pi_{i}(i)+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi_{i}(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi_{i};1,i)

(802)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi(1)+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;1,i)

(803)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi(i)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;i)

(804)

and (
799
) follows. Now, we show that (
800
) reduces to (
799
) via the automorphism
\pi\mapsto\pi^{-1}
of
\mathcal{S}_{[n]}.
First, note that for any
\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}
we have

Q_{R}(\pi;\pi^{-1}(k_{1}),\cdots,\pi^{-1}(k_{\ell}))=Q_{R}(\pi^{-1};k_{1},\cdots,k_{\ell}).

(805)

Hence, the left hand side of (
800
) may be rewritten as

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(j)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;\pi^{-1}(j))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(j)+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;\pi^{-1}(j),\pi^{-1}(1))

(806)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(j)+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi^{-1};j,1).

(807)

Further, the bijection
\pi\mapsto\pi^{-1}
yields that

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(j)+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi^{-1};j,1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi^{-1})\mathcal{R}_{\pi(j)+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;j,1)

(808)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi(j)+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;j,1).

(809)

Combining (
807
) and (
809
), we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi^{-1}(j)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;\pi^{-1}(j))=\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_{[n]}}\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)\mathcal{R}_{\pi(j)+1}\mathcal{R}_{\pi(1)+1}Q_{R}(\pi;j,1),

(810)

i.e., the left hand side of (
800
) is equal to the left hand side of (
799
) with
j
in place of
i.
As (
799
) holds, (
800
) holds too. Therefore,
\mathfrak{S}_{3}=0.
This concludes the proof that the coefficient
a_{R}^{n,d_{n}-1}
is equal to
\det\bm{M}_{R,n}.
